Packers pick off Drake Maye, jump out to 7-0 lead

📅 2026-11-08  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

The Packers are out to a quick lead in New England.

Drake Maye threw an interception on his first pass of the day and the Packers turned it into their first points of the day. Jordan Love scrambled for a 13-yard score and the Packers are up 7-0 with just over two minutes off the clock.

Maye’s pass for Mack Hollins was snagged by Packers safety Xavier McKinney near midfield and he returned it inside the Patriots’ 15-yard-line. Love scored two plays later and the Patriots’ second drive didn’t go much better.

A short run and an incompletion were followed by an Isaiah McDuffie sack, so the Patriots will need to figure out something different if they want to keep this one from turning into a Packers runaway.

‘Lanterns’ Star’s New Prime Video Hit Is Officially Tumbling Down the Streaming Charts

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Ever since its mid-August debut, Damon Lindelof and Tom King's adaptation of the Green Lantern comics has proved a major hit for HBO. After earning 9.3 million global viewers in its first three days, the series grew in popularity throughout the season, averaging 19.3 million global viewers per episode and growing by 13% from premiere to finale. Key to the show's success were lead performances from Kyle Chandler and Aaron Pierre, who starred as longtime superhero Hal and newcomer John.

For fans of Pierre, Lanterns isn't the only recent project of his to be impressing on the streaming charts. Released on Prime Video in October, following its Toronto International Film Festival debut the month prior, Love of Your Life has proven a big hit with viewers, scoring mostly positive reviews from audiences on Rotten Tomatoes. Directed by Rachel Morrison and written by Julia Cox, the film stars Margaret Qualley as Maya, whose happy marriage to Gabriel Basso's Charlie changes forever when he loses his life in the COVID-19 pandemic.

Later in the film, whilst struggling to rebuild her life, Qualley's Maya meets Felix, played by Lanterns' Pierre, who helps her discover hope in her darkness. Patrick Schwarzenegger, Catherine Keener, Tamara Lawrance, and Corey Johnson also feature. A month on from its streaming debut, Love of Your Life is still clinging to its place among the top ten. At the time of writing, the romantic drama ranks sixth on Prime Video in the U.S., with the swampland-set thriller HUNGRY topping the charts. Other popular titles currently on Prime Video include the new comedy Ali G: Who Iz I?, the Mia Farrow-led Forget Me Never, and the rom-com The Love Hypothesis.

Love of Your Life isn't just a hit on the streaming charts. Following its TIFF debut, critics were positive in their response, including Collider's Tania Hussain, who said, "It’s not going to break new ground in terms of romantic dramas, but Qualley and the people around her make sure it still leaves a mark." The film is "certified fresh" on review aggregator Rotten Tomatoes, and was called "emotionally mature," "tone-rich," and "beautiful" by other critics.

Love of Your Life is available to stream now on Prime Video. Stay tuned to Collider for the latest streaming stories.

Giants get first win in Philadelphia since 2013

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Sunday’s performance in Philadelphia wasn’t a work of art for the Giants, but it was more than good enough to end their long losing streak in the City of Brotherly Love.

Tight end Isaiah Likely’s touchdown catch in the third quarter gave the Giants their first lead since the first quarter and they expanded it to 19-11 before punting the ball back to the Eagles with 90 seconds left to play in the game. Joe Milton picked up first downs on completions to Hollywood Brown and Dallas Goedert, but rookie wideout Makai Lemon dropped a pass that would have moved them into Giants territory and running back Elijah Mitchell didn’t get out of bounds on a short catch on the next play.

Milton spiked the ball with six seconds left to play and the Eagles on their own 44-yard-line for one final heave. Milton famously uncorked a 70-yard throw at the Combine, but he never got a chance to throw the ball because he was sacked by defensive end Darius Alexander.

That was one of seven Giants sacks on the afternoon, including three from first-round pick Arvell Reese. It was Reese’s first road game against the Eagles and his 1-0 record in Philly is a big change from recent history. The Giants had lost 13 straight games in Philadelphia and the route to ending that skid featured some strange twists and turns.

Likely’s touchdown catch came on a throw from Jameis Winston, who replaced J.J. McCarthy after McCarthy hurt his hip in the third quarter. Winston played one full series and threw for 71 yards on his way to the touchdown, but McCarthy returned just before the end of the quarter and led the offense for the rest of the proceedings.

McCarthy was sacked five times, but was 12-of-19 for 127 yards when he had time to throw and he ran for 22 yards while evading pressure over the course of the afternoon. The Giants couldn’t find any other success on the ground and Sunday’s 243 yards of offense didn’t make a strong case for the Giants sitting out the trade deadline if they have a chance to acquire any kind of help for their backfield.

Wide receiver Malik Nabers had five catches for 43 yards, but dropped a pass in the end zone that would have iced the win and his body language was that of an unhappy player throughout the game. The win should make it easier to avoid it being a focal point for the team after the game, but Nabers will likely have to answer some questions about what was bugging him.

They’ll be back on the field Thursday night against the Commanders and another win would move them to 6-2-1 with a 3-0-1 record in the division. That would make them even heavier favorites to win an NFC East that is theirs for the taking.

The Eagles spent a lot of time in that position in recent years, but injuries have gutted the roster and Milton didn’t provide any significant upgrade on the offensive production that the team got with Tanner McKee at quarterback. The Eagles will have a bye week to determine which way they want to go in the future, but the 2-7 team should really only have eyes on 2027 at this point.

Broncos beat Panthers 26-21 for fifth consecutive win

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

It is rarely pretty, but the Broncos keep finding ways to win.

The Broncos were outgained by the Panthers 353 to 303, went 2-for-4 in the red zone, including a red-zone interception. They still won their fifth consecutive game, beating Carolina 26-21 to move to 7-2. The Panthers are 3-5.

The Panthers led 14-13 into the fourth quarter, but the Broncos outscored them 13-0 before Carolina gave itself a chance for a comeback on Tetairoa McMillan’s 4-yard touchdown reception. The Broncos got the ball back with 2:59 left with a chance to run out the time, but on second down, Bo Nix’s pitch to Jonah Coleman was fumbled backward for a 12-yard loss.

That gave Carolina a chance to win it with 1:49 remaining.

The Panthers got 20 yards to the Denver 36 before Nik Bonitto had a strip-sack of Bryce Young that the edge rusher recovered with 1:18 left.

That gave Nix his 17th consecutive win in one-score games, the longest streak in the NFL since 1950.

Nix was 25-of-37 for 237 yards with two touchdowns and an interception. Courtland Sutton caught a 3-yard score and Pat Bryant had a 26-yard touchdown reception. Coleman ran for a 9-yard touchdown.

Bryant caught four passes for 56 yards and Coleman had 17 runs for 57 yards.

The Panthers had 13 penalties for 119 yards, had two turnovers and Ryan Fitzgerald missed a 43-yard field goal.

Young was 28-of-39 for 276 yards with three touchdowns and an interception. Chuba Hubbard had 16 carries for 79 yards and Darren Waller had eight catches for 90 yards and a touchdown.

Myles Garrett picks up 3.0 sacks in return, Rams beat Commanders 20-13

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

The star edge rusher returned from a knee procedure and showed the difference he can make, racking up 3.0 sacks in the Rams’ 20-13 victory over the Commanders on Sunday afternoon.

Garrett’s first sack also forced a fumble, as he poked the ball out from quarterback Jayden Daniels in the second quarter. That led to a field goal late in the half, which cut Washington’s lead to 10-6 at halftime.

Garrett then had two more in the fourth quarter, one of which came after he’d just been flagged for an offside penalty.

While Garrett starred on defense, receiver Puka Nacua paced the offense with 14 catches for 150 yards with a touchdown. His 14 receptions are the third-most he’s had in a single game in his career. He also had a pair of carries for 20 yards.

The Commanders had a 10-6 lead at halftime from a 37-yard field goal and a 7-yard touchdown pass from Jayden Daniels to Van Jefferson. The Rams got two field goals from Brandon McManus for their six points.

But the Rams were able to double up with a touchdown on their first drive of the second half. Blake Corum ran it in for a 1-yard score to cap an eight-play, 69-yard drive. And while a Matthew Stafford interception allowed the Commanders to tie the game at 13-13, Stanford then threw a touchdown to Nacua to put the Rams ahead for good.

Washington had a chance to at least tie the game late, but time ran out on their last drive.

Daniels finished the game 24-of-35 for 273 yards with a touchdown. He had four carries for 16 yards.

Stafford finished 26-of-43 for 228 yards with a TD and a pick.

At 5-4, the Rams are now above .500 for the first time this season and on a three-game winning streak. The team will be on the road to face division-rival Arizona next weekend before a Week 11 bye.

The Commanders have fallen to 2-5-1. They won’t have much time to think about their missed opportunities, as they’ll face the Giants on Thursday night in Week 10.

Recasting Sabrina The Teenage Witch In 2026: All 9 Main Roles

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

In 1996, Melissa Joan Hart perfectly embodied the titular star of the magical coming-of-age sitcom, Sabrina the Teenage Witch, but 30 years on, a reboot could have a very different cast. The show was a massive success, and as such, it ended up running for seven seasons from 1996 until 2003.

Beyond that, the series also ended up with an animated spin-off and sequel, a reimagined revival in 2018 which took a much darker approach to the narrative, and a handful of feature films, including the 1996 movie starring Hart and Ryan Reynolds that kicked it all off.

But, if TV executives wanted to bring the series back today, and stick more closely to the light-hearted comedy roots of the 1996 series, there are only a handful of actors who could really excel at playing the parts that brought this series to life in the first place.

First up, we need a loud, boisterous, and versatile actor who can bring the humor, enthusiasm, and depth to the character of Sabrina that this role deserves. And with that in mind, there is no one better suited to playing this part than Ruby Stokes. Stokes has been steadily building out her credits since 2010, with a major turn in 2016 when she played a central role in the film Una.

Since then, Stokes has tackled a variety of performances, from the stage to the screen, and performed in period dramas, dark comedies, and captivating dramas, all of which have given her both the experience to play the role and more than qualified her to take the lead as a mischievous young witch discovering her powers.

While Gillian Jacobs played the uptight and anxious Britta in Community, the role that she is currently best known for, she also has the capacity to play much more laid-back and fun-loving roles. And considering Hilda Spellman, one of Sabrina's aunts who is also a full witch, is prone to act on impulse and take things less seriously than her sister Zelda, it would make a lot of sense to cast Jacobs as Hilda.

Hilda is the younger sister who loves to have fun and is willing to play along with Sabrina's ideas, often getting herself in trouble with her sister along with their niece. Jacobs brings humor, vibrancy, and warmth to her roles that allows her to deliver characters who may not be the most practical, but they are certainly endearing.

Meanwhile, Zelda Spellman is frequently the voice of reason, and the most level-headed witch in the room. In order to bring this part to life, someone like Ayo Edebiri, who recently came off her turn in the incredible series, The Bear, would be an ideal choice to play the person keeping everything together while everyone else is chasing their own directions.

In many ways, this is exactly how her character, Sydney, ended her run in The Bear, having learned valuable lessons through her challenging work with the team at The Bear restaurant, before taking the reins and becoming the Head Chef and co-owner. So, in a sense, playing Zelda could help to see that type of character evolve even further in Edebiri's capable hands.

Bill Hader is an accomplished comedy actor whose deadpan delivery style has made him a fan favorite throughout his career, from his time as a regular cast member on SNL to a series of roles in TV and film over the last decade or so. Hader has also taken to voice acting in recent years with a growing list of credits for major roles in projects such as The Cat in The Hat, Inside Out, and many more.

With all of that preparation, Hader is easily the strongest candidate to tackle the sarcastic and snappy wizard who was turned into a cat as a punishment for attempting to take over the world, Salem.

Harvey Winkle is the awkward but lovable, goofy and endearing love interest that Sabrina Spellman finds herself fawning over. While the character in the 1996 series was popular thanks to his status as an athlete, times have moved on, and there are many other qualities that earn people a stamp of popularity and approval in high school settings, which opens the door for an updated take on Harvey, played by Jack Martin.

Martin has only recently started to land credited roles on the screen with his career taking off in 2020. Since then, he has tackled a number of small parts in TV and film, before landing a central role in the 2026 comedy series, Not Suitable For Work. It's here that Martin proved himself as the perfect cast for a part like Harvey Kinkle, and with that series getting axed after its debut season, Martin has some space on his schedule.

Libby Chessler is the quintessentially popular girl in a high school setting who is the head of the cheerleading squad, and who is frequently seen belittling and mocking other girls for being weird, or as Libby liberally refers to her classmates such as Sabrina, "freaks."

As an athlete, it makes sense to cast a performer like Maddie Ziegler who can bring the physicality to the role to convincingly play this part, while also having a cold and intense outward face that could inspire fear in others and establish her as a dominant force.

On the back of his performance as Phil Dunphy in Modern Family, Ty Burrell is an outstanding actor who brings so much physicality, personality, and incredible humor to his roles. All of that would go a long way to depicting one of the most fascinating mainstays of the original show, Principal Willard Kraft.

Principal Kraft is a bit of a bumbling buffoon who has an intense crush on Sabrina's aunt Hilda, but throughout the show's run ends up sparking relationships with both aunts at different points. Burrell would be an incredible addition to the lineup, and one that could reasonably juggle all of the different facets of the character that becomes so integral throughout the show's run.

In season 1 of Sabrina the Teenage Witch, Sabrina has a best friend by the name of Jenny Kelley. Jenny is quirky, studious, and eccentric, which means she matches Sabrina's energy as a young witch without knowing her friend's newfound secret. Together, the pair get up to all sorts of misadventures, and they appear to have a powerful bond that brings them together.

However, the series mysteriously replaced the character from season 2 onward. Regardless, a reboot could bring Jenny Kelley back into the story with an actor like Anna Cathcart who delivered an outstanding performance in To All the Boys and XO, Kitty, making her a more central and enduring part of the narrative. Cathcart perfectly embodies the traits of this character, and would make a great contrast to the energy that Ruby Stokes could bring to Sabrina.

In seasons 2 and 3, Valerie Birkhead takes Jenny's place as Sabrina's best friend, and the show avoids referencing Jenny ever again. Valerie then asserts herself as the new best friend, though the energy she brings to the part is much more self-conscious, shy, and awkward. And in a reboot, it would be great to have Sabrina matched up with both Jenny and Valerie to flesh out the cast and give the character more to bounce off.

So, to tackle this role, an experienced comedy actor with a talent for playing more reserved and quiet roles makes the most sense, which is why Iris Apatow is a perfect fit. Apatow has perfected the portrayal of teenage neuroticism that is central to playing the role of Valerie, and her presence opposite Ruby Stokes would be a pleasure to watch unfold.

All in all, while an updated take on Sabrina the Teenage Witch would no doubt look very different to what came before, the talent to pull off such a reboot is perfectly positioned to make an updated version of the series that captures the magic of the original.

Jennifer Garner Details "Incredible" Chapter in Ben Affleck Marriage

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Jennifer Garner pulled back the curtain on a sweet chapter during her marriage to Ben Affleck when she took the pair’s three kids on a European vacation: “It was wonderful.”

Jennifer Garner is thinking back to a time when she was thriving during her marriage to Ben Affleck.

The 13 Going on 30 alum detailed the unforgettable vacation she took with her and the Gone Girl star’s kids Violet, 20, Fin, 17, and Samuel, 14, before calling it quits with Affleck in 2015.

“When my kids were really little, I went to Paris,” Garner told Ina Garten on a recent episode of her Happy Hour podcast. “Ben was shooting some Batman-ish movie in London and we went for six weeks. I took them out of school. It was the last time I really took kids out of school, but it was wonderful. It was incredible. It was so fun.”

“And part of that is that we bopped around,” Garner recalled. “We went to Venice, we went to Paris.”

And while the Batman v Superman: Dawn of Justice actor was busy getting into character as Bruce Wayne, it was Garten who suggested the Alias actress explore the French city by boat.

Since then, it’s turned into a full-blown tradition for the Daredevil actress. How fabulous is that?

“I’ve done it now, every time I go,” Garner said. “Every picture from that night, from that trip, the smile on my kids’ faces—it was such an adventure that you gave us.”

Garner and Affleck, both 54, tied the knot in 2005 before separating in 2015. And while they finalized their divorce in 2018, they’ve remained a team in co-parenting their little ones.

In fact, The Town actor recently gave his ex her flowers in the parenting department, calling her a “spectacular” mom on the Oct. 11 episode of Sunday Today with Willie Geist before admitting “it always makes me feel like I’m coming up a little short.”

And he’s not afraid to let his kids know that either, sharing he tells them that he’s “doing about 75 percent as good as your mom.”

“And they’re like, ‘Maybe 40 percent,’” he added with a laugh. “I’m maxing out. Woo, B+!”

But in all seriousness, Affleck expressed that he couldn’t be more “grateful” to have a co-parent like the Juno actress.

“I respect her,” he emphasized. “I think the most important thing is having that mutual respect—being able to get along with that person.”

Keep reading for more of Hollywood’s friendliest exes...

No, they are never, ever, ever getting back together. But at this point—between the birthday parties, the friendly embraces and even freaking acting out a sexual fantasy in a star-studded virtual reading of Fast Times at Ridgemont High—it feels like the former Hollywood golden couple is just taunting us.

You know you're cool when you can pop into your ex's talk show and give him a hard time. Such was the case when Silverman turned up on Kimmel's stage in 2018 to chat about her show I Love You, America and his "cute," "bright," "funny" wife Molly McNearney. "It's not fair!" the actress cracked. "She gets, like, new woke Jimmy. I had Man Show Jimmy."

TBH, we might still be mourning this relationship harder than either of its participants. Because when Faris—now married to cinematographer Michael Barrett—heard that Pratt was set to wed Katherine Schwarzenegger in 2019, she offered to officiate. She even sent over a gift after the newlyweds welcomed daughter Lyla in August 2020. Thrilled for son Jack to become a big brother, a source told E! News at the time that Faris "is very supportive."

It would have been super easy for the exes' relationship to become a flop. But more than four years after a messy split, the HGTV stars are still killing it at two projects: parenting kids Taylor and Brayden and turning run-down properties into someone else's dream home.

Literally "best friends" as the rocker put it in a 2013 interview with Oprah Winfrey, the parents to actress Zoe Kravitz still consider themselves kin. "We're one big happy family," Lenny said of his ex, who went on to wed Jason Momoa before their 2020 split. "It's beautiful, and it just shows you what can be done."

After the Dancing With the Stars co-host and former hockey player called in quits in 2020, the pair have continually proved they are on good terms.

In 2025, Hough enjoyed a trip to the lake with Laich and his then-pregnant fiancée Katrín Tanja Davíðsdóttir.

And when Laich announced his marriage to the former Crossfit athlete in 2025, Hough celebrated the news, writing on Instagram, “Congratulations! So so happy for you both. Sending you all the love, health and happiness for the years to come to you and your beautiful family."

Since their 2015 breakup, the parents to Violet, Seraphina and Samuel have hit the conscious uncoupling trifecta: Joint vacations, shared holidays and public displays of admiration with Ben dubbing his ex "an amazing mother" during a 2016 interview with E! News.

Affleck isn't the only one of Garner's exes who she's stayed close with after breakup. She's also friends with her former Alias costar, who she briefly dated in 2003 before her marriage to Affleck.

"I can’t remember why I was Bavarian, but I do know today is your birthday," Garner wrote on Instagram in 2023 along a still from Alias, in which she was dressed in a dirndl. "I hope it’s a great one, Michael! XX."

The Vampire Diaries costars may have called it quits in 2013 after three years of dating, but they've remained close friends since then. In 2014, somerhalder started dating Nikki Reed and tied the knot later that year. Naturally, Dobrev befriended the Twilight actress.

The two called it quits in July 2016 after five years together. The singer said on SiriusXM's The Howard Stern Show the following October that she's "very close" to Kinney, adding, "He's been my lover and a friend for so long."

"We love each other," she said. "That's it."

The Office's on-and-off pairing remained so tantalizingly close after the series wrapped in 2013 that even Kaling gets why people are still shipping them. "If I was on the outside looking at it, I would think the same thing," she told Good Housekeeping in 2019 of naming him godparent to her kids. "But the truth is that B.J. is so much more like family now than a platonic friend."

The exes had no trouble coparenting their son Bronx after finalizing their divorce back in 2011. The Fall Out Boy bassist has even said his former wife is "an awesome mom."

So she's over those "7 Things" then? The Jonas Brothers musician has said he's "really friendly" with the "Wrecking Ball" singer. "We've got a good relationship."

The Dexter stars first met on set in 2006 before things got romantic in 2008. And though they divorced in 2011, Carpenter assured there was "nothing but love and respect" between the exes a few years later.

Long the gold standard of copacetic exes, the parents to daughters Rumer, Tallulah and Scout remain so tight that the decision to ride out coronavirus stay-at-home orders together in Idaho was a no-brainer. Quarantine dance party anyone?

Some four years after their 2014 divorce, Lopez was still giving her former spouse—dad to her twins Max and Emme—props. "While I was married to Marc, he really helped me with my confidence and he saw where I suffered," she said on a 2018 episode of Sunday Today. "He was like, 'You're a great singer. Don't ever let anybody tell you you're not a great singer.' And I respected him so much 'cause I consider him one of the best singers of all time."

Even if it hasn't been his day, his week, his month or even his year, the actress will be there for her former husband and Scream costar. "I mean, as divorces go, I would say it's one of the better ones. For sure," she shared on Running Wild With Bear Grylls eight years after their 2010 split. Raising daughter Coco, she continued, "We're good co-parents together."

Their real-life romance may had fizzled in 2007, but that didn't mean the two couldn't heat up the screen in the 2011 movie Bad Teacher.

Sure, the initial few months of their 2013 split were rough going, but the two have now reached the group vacation stage of their divorce. "I really like her husband and I think he's a great guy," the Twilight alum told LaPalme Magazine of a 2017 trip to Cancun that included Garth, Dave Abrams, Peter's now-fiancée Lily Anne Harrison and their three daughters Luca, Lola and Fiona.

As for his relationship with Garth, he continued, "People come into your life for a reason and relationships can change," he told LaPalme. "Just because we aren't living together doesn't mean you can't love each other."

Despite a surprise separation in September 2012, the Blades of Glory costars decided to put on a united front for their children. "As you know, Will and I are very, very good friends," Poehler explained during a 2014 interview with Howard Stern. "We are doing a really good job. I don't think a 10-year marriage constitutes a failure. I think relationships are really tough."

The Grammy winner and songwriter ex—who finalized their divorce in January 2024—have remained on good terms as they continue to parent their son Hayes. In fact, the pair live just five minutes apart from one another, which makes co-parenting a breeze.

“Our stops are really easy,” Morris told The Zoe Report in May 2025, “and we’ll have family dinners.”

Chiefs crush Jets 35-12 to end two-game losing streak

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Kenneth Walker had a big hand in the Chiefs’ five-game winning streak to open the season and he had a big hand in ending their two-game losing streak on Sunday.

Walker caught a pair of touchdown passes from Patrick Mahomes in the first half of the game and he ran for another score in the second half of Kansas City’s 35-12 home win over the Jets. The victory moves the Chiefs to 6-2 on the season and Walker is now tied with Jahmyr Gibbs for the league lead with 11 total touchdowns on the season.

It’s the fifth multi-touchdown game of Walker’s first season with the Chiefs and the Chiefs have won all of those games. He did not score in either of their losses, so his importance to the offense probably can’t be overstated.

Walker ran 18 times for 102 yards and his four catches picked up 41 yards, which gave him more yards from scrimmage on Sunday than he managed in either of the Chiefs’ two losses.

The Chiefs got a scare just before halftime when Patrick Mahomes hurt his right elbow on a sack by Jets first-rounder David Bailey. The Chiefs lost a fumble on the play, but Mahomes was able to remain in the game and no negative impact on his game was visible over the second half. Mahomes finished 23-of-31 for 242 yards and four of those completions went to Travis Kelce for 72 yards. Kelce also had a one-yard rushing touchdown.

Mahomes and Kelce have been joined on the top line of Chiefs stars by defensive tackle Chris Jones for many years and Jones chipped in a pair of sacks of Jets quarterback Geno Smith during the win. George Karlaftis had a pair of them as well and the Chiefs will try to keep things firing on all cylinders in Atlanta next Sunday.

One change they could make is with kicker Harrison Butker. He missed another field goal and Justin Tucker is on the practice squad, so the team could opt for a new kicker against the Falcons.

Smith was sacked five times . The Jets actually controlled much of the first half and had chances to put points on the board, but had to settle for five field goal tries. Jason Sanders made four of those kicks, but the Jets needed touchdowns that never came.

They will host the Bills next Sunday and the 2-7 team figures to hear a lot more chatter about Aaron Glenn’s job security in the wake of their fourth straight loss. Five of their first six losses were one-score games, but getting outscored 21-0 in the second half on Sunday doesn’t make much of a case for Glenn having the team on the right path.

‘Avedon’ Review: Ron Howard’s Thrilling Documentary About Richard Avedon, the Great Photographer Who Turned Head-On Portraits Into an Art Form

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Avedon was a paragon of high fashion and also the ultimate democratic humanist. The film explains that paradox and makes his images sing.

“Avedon,” Ron Howard’s documentary about the legendary photographer, is a fantastic movie. It’s the best documentary Ron Howard has made (my favorite before this: “The Beatles: Eight Days a Week”), and it’s been edited in a way that’s so supple and artful that its technique should be studied in filmmaking classes. (The film’s editor is Andrew Morreale.) Woven through the movie are hundreds of photographs that were shot by Richard Avedon, and there’s no question that the images are stunning, but the way the movie layers them into your consciousness is nearly musical in its finesse. And that helps to tell the very large story “Avedon” is telling: how Richard Avedon changed the metaphysics of photography, shifting the way we see the world. Related Stories Paramount Network Orders 13 Original Episodes of Larry the Cable Guy-Hosted Clip Show 'American Mayhem' (EXCLUSIVE)

There’s a paradox running down the center of Avedon’s work that feels as epic and tingly as the 20th century, and that is this: He was a paragon of high fashion — the world of glamour and impossible beauty — but he was also, as a maker of images, the ultimate democratic humanist. The beauty of “Avedon” is that it explains that paradox and makes those images sing. Howard interpolates old clips and interviews, placing Avedon, with his sleek command, at the center of the movie. He also talks to models and critics and media and art-world luminaries who have a great deal of import to say. They include Tina Brown, Larry Gagosian, John Lahr, Twiggy, Adam Gopnik, Hilton Als, Calvin Klein, and Avedon’s lovingly insightful son, John Avedon. James Martin, the executive director of the Richard Avedon Foundation, takes us deep into the labyrinth of Avedon’s archives, where we see the work that resulted from the 16,000 sittings he did from 1944 until his death in 2004. That’s a long time, but it still averages out to one sitting per day, Monday through Friday, for 60 years. The movie also tells the tragic story of his mentally ill sister, whom he revered, and traces his troubled marriages.

“Avedon” opens with an old clip of Richard Avedon, fixing that piercing bird-eye look of his into the camera as he explains, “I can say very little about photography. I follow no rules, no laws, and no advice.” He’s totally serious about that. We then get a montage, set to the funky martial beat of Spoon’s “I Turn My Camera On,” of Avedon portraits of the famous. John Lennon. Orson Welles. Michael Jordan. Elizabeth Taylor. Groucho Marx. Twiggy (in mid-air). Anjelica Huston. Marilyn Monroe lovingly embracing Arthur Miller. Truman Capote. J. Robert Oppenheimer.

All the images look timeless. (Avedon’s lighting makes it look as if each one was taken yesterday.) Every subject looks like what we imagine them looking like, and also different from how you’ve ever seen them. More direct and unfiltered, more intimately themselves. That’s the Avedon touch, but the magic element is that we can’t see how he achieved it. The technique is invisible. There’s a part of one that thinks, “Okay, just set up a camera and put some black-and-white film in it. Place a famous person in front of it. Encourage them to relax and ‘be themselves.’ And voilà! I could be Richard Avedon.”

He probably would have said you’re not wrong.

He wasn’t very interested in the technique of photography. Talk to him about f-stops and he would glaze over. His technique was in the way he approached a session. Iin the film, Isabella Rossellini says that other photographers tend to shoot and shoot and shoot, but Avedon would shoot an image every so often, then tell you to think of something, then shoot again, and end up with a total of maybe 10 shots. He himself explains that if you’ve booked someone for an hour, you don’t have to shoot for an hour. He says that he wastes half an hour having coffee, and then, maybe five minutes before the hour is over, he and the subject will go over to the white paper background, and the magic happens.

The white paper! It has its source in a remarkable story. Avedon was born in New York in 1923, and when he was a kid he says that walking down a street he would blink for a moment and keep his eyes closed, to keep what he saw alive in his head. It was his way of taking pictures before he’d even gotten a camera. He entered the U.S. Merchant Marines when he was 19, securing a place in the photographic department, where he took maybe 100,000 portraits of enlisted men and other workers, all to use for ID cards.

This was the photographer’s equivalent of peeling potatoes on KP duty. But Avedon began to notice something. Shooting these military mug shots against a white background, he encountered “thousands of baffled faces looking into the camera,” and he saw how the photographs revealed these people. That’s one of the startling things about police mug shots. (John Waters: “Everybody looks better under arrest.”) We see a handful of the ID photos that Avedon took, and there it is: the Avedon style. He saw that each face was different, and needed nothing else to be different. So he took what fate handed him — a thankless military assignment — and turned it into a vision. The white background equalized everything. It became his way of making each individual stand out as an individual, with literally nothing to get in the way.

He became a head-on existential classicist, one who echoed Diane Arbus’s eye for highlighting the aspect of a person that was somehow off. The famous Avedon shot of the model Lauren Hutton with her head thrown back and her breast popping out looks as perfectly composed as a Picasso sculpture. But it was totally spontaneous; it just happened. And that’s true of all the famous and iconic gazes Avedon’s portrait subjects gave him. Those looks weren’t planned (how could they have been?).

At the tail-end of a session in 1953, Charlie Chaplin pranked the camera by holding devil horns over his grinning face, and that became one of the most famous images Avedon ever shot. Images like that happened…and then became frozen in the hive mind’s eye. But as “Avedon” explains, part of what he was always recording was the ineffable dance between subject and artist, using each subject to express something about himself. (Chaplin wouldn’t have made those devil horns for anyone else.)

By the time of the 1957 Hollywood musical “Funny Face,” which paired Audrey Hepburn with Fred Astaire as a magazine photographer pointedly based on Avedon, he was already becoming a legend, and he began to use his power as part of his method as an artist. In his 1957 shoot with Marilyn Monroe, pegged to “The Prince and the Showgirl,” the trick was: How could you photograph Marilyn — or, rather, how could you photograph her the way Avedon wanted to photograph her — when she had come prepared, more than anyone else alive, to be photographed? The shoot took an entire day, and it’s full of joyous photographs of Marilyn in full splendor, but the iconic shot that emerged from it was taken at the end of the day, when she let down her guard for 10 seconds, and kind of drooped, and looked a bit sad, and we glimpse, in that photograph, the real Marilyn — not necessarily the forlorn Marilyn, but the full woman behind the image. You see Norma Jean grown up.

“Avedon” is full of great stories and privileged glimpses into how Avedon created some of his most famous images. And, of course, he also photographed the unfamous, like the Oklahoma oil workers he shot in 1980. He used his impish nature to coax fashion models into leaping through the air for his models-in-motion photographs. He waited around for seven weeks in Vietnam to photograph the war’s American generals, and devised singular ways to shoot the Chicago 7 and the denizens of the Warhol Factory and the residents of the new American West, in a series that was so raw and authentic no museum in New York wanted anything to do with it. (He was that far ahead of the curve,) We also hear the story of how he photographed his aging father and used that process to reconcile with him. Avedon supported his own ritzy lifestyle by doing advertising work (including the Obsession perfume TV spots that were like surrealist mini-films), but for him it was never a compromise. And we see how that could be the case. There is, over 60 years, a breathtaking continuity between his portrait work and his fashion work and his commercial work that makes it all inseparable. That’s because every photograph Richard Avedon ever took was striving for the same thing: to open your eyes.

Finally, CBS Confronts Painful Blue Bloods Truth With Boston Spinoff

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Ana Dumaraog is a Writer and Editor at Screen Rant, where she expertly covers Network TV offerings, among other things She has spearheaded the effort to make the site an authority on broadcast shows, initially starting with The Big Bang Theory franchise, NCIS, and One Chicago before taking on the specialization in general. Ana has also covered a wide-spread of topics in the past — from franchises like, the MCU and Star Wars, to classic TV favorites, such as Frasier and MASH.

Ana brings clarity and enthusiasm to every story she tackles—whether reporting on new releases or offering deep dives into fan-favorite franchises. Her coverage translates complex entertainment developments into engaging, audience-focused content.

Warning! Spoilers ahead for Boston Blue season 2, episode 5, "Blood Diamond."Nearly two years after Blue Bloods ended its run, CBS finally confronts the painful truth about it with regard to Boston Blue. For a decade and a half, the Reagan family became a staple of the network's yearly slate, with the Tom Selleck-led series becoming a fan-favorite. Unlike the traditional police procedural, Blue Bloods differentiated itself by making it a family show first and foremost. The project dealt with a group of New York public officials, serving different agencies in the city.

After evading cancellation a couple of times, CBS officially cancelled the series in 2024, with Blue Bloods ending with season 14. The final stretch of the Reagans' run on TV was full of compelling cases and major personal milestones for the clan. It left viewers with the idea that while the series was wrapping, the Reagans' life in New York was going to continue. That was until it was announced that CBS was launching a spinoff with Donny Wahlberg's Danny spearheading Boston Blue. The offshoot has been a success thus far, and both Blue Bloods actors and their characters have been supportive of the expansion project. That said, it has always just been a matter of time before the long-ignored truth about Boston Blue comes to light.

Boston Blue has brought in multiple Reagans throughout the course of its run thus far. That said, its return during the CBS's fall 2026 slate marks the first time that it is featuring Will Estes as Jamie Reagan. The youngest of Frank's kids makes a trip up north to see how his older brother is doing after moving out of New York. Unlike Danny's interactions with Erin, Henry, and even Joe Hill, this latest reunion is tense, as Jamie finally confronts Wahlberg's character about leaving New York, and in effect, him.

Tackling this particularly difficult topic is long-overdue in Boston Blue. Granted that Sean's near-death experience was a justified reason for Danny to follow his son to Beantown, the offshoot arguably glossed over the specifics of his decision to not just stay with Sean temporarily, but uproot his whole life to be up north. For a clan that likes to discuss everything, Jamie reveals in his Boston Blue appearance that Danny barely talked to him about leaving. Blue Bloods was a family show; while the spinoff offered details about their conversations about the move, it didn't offer the whole picture, as it's difficult to imagine that it was an easy transition for the whole Regan clan. It took CBS a while, but thankfully, CBS finally addresses the matter.

It's no secret that Blue Bloods' cancellation was quite controversial. CBS had tried pulling the plug on the series a few times, but the cast and crew rallied to keep the series on the air. Despite the family procedural's popularity during its run, the network's budget cuts motivated them to cancel the series, which was expensive to make, considering that it was filming primarily on location in New York. Still, CBS recognized the popularity of the series and found a way to continue taking advantage of it, which resulted in Boston Blue.

The Raegans are preparing to say goodbye as Blue Bloods heads to its final episodes. Luckily, network TV already has a perfect 100% on Rotten Tomatoes

Considering the core message of Blue Bloods, splitting up the Reagans was the biggest hurdle when justifying the existence of Boston Blue. For over a decade and a half, the family came together every Sunday for family dinner, so seeing Danny leave would have been a major change for the clan. Jamie's debut in Boston Blue offers viewers an idea of how painful that transition was, even as the matter was originally ignored. That said, considering that his move was also for family, it's easier to accept it.

Blue Bloods follows the Reagan family, a multigenerational dynasty dedicated to law enforcement and public service in New York City. As Police Commissioner Frank Reagan and his children serve as a detective, patrol officer, prosecutor, and investigator, each episode balances the demands of policing with the family's personal lives.

Boston Blue follows NYPD officer Danny Reagan as he transitions to a role in the Boston Police Department. Partnered with detective Lena Peters, the eldest daughter of a notable law enforcement family, the series explores their collaboration within the dynamics of Boston's criminal landscape.

The Sci-Fi Masterpiece That Ridley Scott Dismissed as Too “Slow” Is Officially Streaming for Free

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

He has covered everything from Marvel to the Oscars, and Marvel at the Oscars. He also writes obsessively about the box office, charting the many hits and misses that are released weekly, and how their commercial performance shapes public perception. In his time at Collider, he has also helped drive diversity by writing stories about the multiple Indian film industries, with a goal of introducing audiences to a whole new world of cinema.

Director Ridley Scott made a disappointing return to the sci-fi genre this year, with the post-apocalyptic movie The Dog Stars. Starring Jacob Elordi in the lead role, the film grossed around $40 million worldwide against a reported budget of approximately $100 million. Around the same time as the film's release and mediocre reception, Scott announced his intention to return to the Alien franchise, which he helped create in the 1970s. The only trouble is that the franchise was going in an entirely different direction, one that Scott clearly wasn't too impressed by. Scott voiced similar reservations about another reboot of one of his most memorable movies. The movie in question was recently added to a free streaming platform.

A couple of years ago, director Fede Álvarez successfully rebooted the Alien series with Alien: Romulus, which grossed $350 million worldwide against a reported budget of $80 million, and received excellent reviews. A sequel to the movie was quickly announced, but some time later, Álvarez revealed that he wouldn't be returning as director. Scott said in an interview that he found the movie to be just "okay," and that he intends to revisit the narrative that he began with Prometheus and Alien: Covenant.

While Prometheus grossed more than $400 million worldwide, Covenant underperformed with around $240 million globally and brought the franchise to a halt. It wasn't until Romulus that the series appeared to have been resurrected. However, with Scott effectively wanting to erase the movie entirely, it remains to be seen where the series will go next. Curiously, Scott made similar comments about Denis Villeneuve's Blade Runner 2049.

Released in 2017, the legacy sequel received phenomenal reviews but underperformed commercially. It ended up grossing only around $280 million worldwide against a reported budget of more than $180 million. However, many found it to be an improvement over Scott's original, the classic sci-fi noir Blade Runner. Scott disagreed with this contingent, saying in an interview that he found Blade Runner 2049 to be too "slow" and too "long."

The movie now holds a "Certified Fresh" 88% score on the aggregator website Rotten Tomatoes, where the consensus reads, "Visually stunning and narratively satisfying, Blade Runner 2049 deepens and expands its predecessor's story while standing as an impressive filmmaking achievement in its own right." The Blade Runner franchise will be rebooted on November 25 with the Prime Video series Blade Runner 2099, starring Hunter Schafer and Michelle Yeoh. Meanwhile, you can revisit Villeneuve's epic on Pluto TV.

This Chilling Japanese Folk Horror Series Is One Of Hulu's Best Hidden Gems

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

If you're in the mood for a discrete dose of folk horror, Hulu recently added Adam Scott's terrifying horror "Hokum." But if you'd like to extend your trauma over, say two seasons of sheer terror, then "Gannibal" is for you. The show stars Yuya Yagira as a police officer who leaves the city to start over in a remote village. Here, he quickly discovers things aren't quite as idyllic as they seem.

"Gannibal" premiered in December 2022 but has failed to make much of an impact outside of Japan, despite arguably being one of the best horror series of the 2020s. Thus far, two seasons of "Gannibal" have been released, and had they hit Netflix in the United States, the show may well have become a big hit stateside. As it stands, this is one of Japan's best kept secrets and it's waiting to be discovered on Hulu.

Masaaki Ninomiya's "Gannibal" manga was originally serialized across thirteen volumes from October 2018 to November 2021. In October of that year, it was announced that "Gannibal" would be adapted as a TV series, with Shinzo Katayama directing from teleplays by Takamasa Oe.Thankfully, it turned out to be a worthy adaptation.

One of the best foreign horror shows streaming right now, "Gannibal" shares much in common with the South Korean horror movie with a 99% Rotten Tomatoes score "The Wailing." Both are set in remote villages and follow a police officer who becomes increasingly menaced by some terrifying ancient evil. But "Gannibal" departs from the movie with its tale of a cannibal conspiracy.

Yuya Yagira plays police officer Daigo Agawa, who after an unfortunate ordeal in Tokyo moves to the small mountain village of Kuge with his wife Yuki Agawa (Riho Yoshioka) and daughter Mashiro Agawa (Kokone Shimizu). At first, this secluded hamlet seems like the ideal refuge from the city and Agawa's fraught past. But things get off to an eerie start as Kuge's previous police officer has mysteriously vanished.

As Agawa and his family become more acquainted with the village and its people they discover the Goto family essentially run the town, and they don't seem like the most charming bunch. Meanwhile, a woman's body is found on the mountain, and while the Gotos claim she was killed by a bear, Agawa notices a human bite mark. Soon, his family's escape starts to turn into a nightmare. As such, this is not a horror TV show you can watch with the whole family. But it definitely deserves your attention regardless.

While you may never have heard of "Gannibal," the series has become a sensation in its native Japan. In April 2025, The Hollywood Reporter reported that "Gannibal" had become the most-viewed title in the country on Disney+ since its second season debuted in March of that year. The show also set new records for the streamer, solidifying its status as a bonafide mega-hit in Japan. In the United States, however, "Gannibal" mostly flew under the radar.

There are no "top critic" reviews for the series on Rotten Tomatoes, and it hasn't shown up on any "best horror series" lists since its 2022 debut. But it has managed to generate a small amount of buzz among non-Japanese viewers. One Redditor described the series as "grim folk horror" and was evidently left "completely wrecked" by it. Elsewhere on Reddit, another user described the series as "like 'Midsommar' meets 'Resident Evil 7'" and praised the "amazing actors, great cinematography, chilling and memorable soundtrack, and an absurdly dark story."

Letterboxd users are similarly impressed. "I just love it," writes one, who goes on to commend the series for its "sharp plot," "creepy characters," and "unsettling vibes," as well as the "splendid cinematography." Another also highlighted how the show is "beautifully shot and incredibly well-acted." Unfortunately, it seems unlikely there will be more to come. "Gannibal" Season 2 was released on March 19, 2025 and that wrapped up the story told in Masaaki Ninomiya's manga. Thankfully, if you are able to binge your way through both seasons of the series, there are plenty of great folk horror movies to tide you over.

This X-Men-Esque '80s Sci-Fi Series Launched Courteney Cox's Career

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

The premise of James D. Parriott's 1985 superhero/comedy series "Misfits of Science," which featured a young Courteney Cox, will sound very familiar to fans of Marvel Comics' X-Men titles.

The series follows a human scientist named Dr. Billy Hayes (Dean Paul Martin), who has taken an interest in what he called "human anomalies," which are people who have been born with a variety of unpredictable superpowers. In the show's pilot episode, he assembles the main cast of powerful castoffs with the intention of studying them, but also helping them come to terms with their supernatural abilities.

The team was small but mighty. It included Dr. Hayes' lab partner, Dr. Lincoln (Kevin Peter Hall), who can shrink for a limited period. Additionally, Mark Thomas Miller plays "Johnny B" Bukowski, a rocker who can suck up electricity and who wears shades to cover his glowing eyes. He can throw lightning bolts.

Most strikingly, Courteney Cox, future star of "Friends" and "Scream," plays Gloria, a troubled teen with "Carrie"-like telekinetic powers. "Misfits of Science" wasn't Cox's first credit: She had appeared in an episode of "As the World Turns" back in 1975, when she was still a kid, and famously danced with Bruce Springsteen in the "Dancing in the Dark" music video in 1984. "Misfits of Science," though, was the first time Cox starred in a TV series.

"Misfits of Science" is not currently available to stream, but resourceful media hunters will be able to find episodes online easily enough. It's a whimsical, lighthearted show that prodded at the conventions of superheroes as much as it vaunted them. It's definitely worth a glance.

"Misfits of Science," it should be noted, only lasted for a single 16-episode season, so it's okay if you don't remember it.

The series does have something of a superhero legacy, as R. Timothy Kring, a.k.a. Tim Kring, wrote an episode of "Misfits of Science," and he would go on to create the hit series "Heroes," the first season of which remains a must-watch (the later seasons, though, less so). Kring's "Misfits" episode, called "Twin Engines," is about a young man (Joel Polis) who is plagued with uncontrollable visions. It's eventually revealed that he was sharing the visions of his long-lost twin brother.

Indeed, the entire show was based on a long series of super-powered shenanigans and investigations. Mostly, the protagonists get involved in run-of-the-mill spy plots. The main characters aren't costumed vigilantes, but they do seem to operate as freelance spies. Famed drummer Mikey Jones appears in the show's pilot as a character with freezing powers named Ice Man. According to Giant Freakin' Robot, that character was abandoned after Marvel Comics "raised objections" over its own Ice Man character. And speaking of Marvel, Courteney Cox's character was very similar to the X-Men's Jean Gray.

Some fun trivia: Shortly after the time "Misfits of Science" was being made, Dr. Lincoln actor Kevin Peter Hall (who played the original Predator in John McTiernan's sci-fi action classic) was in contention to play either Data or Geordi La Forge on "Star Trek: The Next Generation." /Film has previously talked about all the finalists for the roles, and Hall eventually lost the roles to Brent Spiner and LeVar Burton, respectively.

As mentioned, "Misfits of Science" was not successful. Thanks to low ratings, the series was canceled before all of its episodes could even air, leaving one of them unaired in the United States.

Courteney Cox continued to work undeterred, of course. In 1987, she appeared in the low-budget, high-profile Cannon adaptation of "Masters of the Universe," a movie so goofy, it has been subjected to a "How Did This Get Made" oral history. That same year, she began playing the recurring role of Lauren Miller on the hit sitcom "Family Ties." By 1994, she was appearing in the comedy "Ace Ventura: Pet Detective" and began playing one of the six lead roles on "Friends." "Misfits of Science" was her first starring role, but it certainly wasn't her last.

Tom Hiddleston's Near-Perfect Apple TV Miniseries Gets Better With Every Episode

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Period dramas span a wide range of shows, including everything from romances to war stories. This variety gives fans almost too many options to watch, and though several have become well known, others inevitably fly under the radar. One such example is Apple TV's The Essex Serpent. The 2022 miniseries is an unpredictable entry in the genre, but even four years out from its release, it's one that period drama fans shouldn't miss.

Adapted from Sarah Perry's novel of the same name, the Apple TV miniseries stars Claire Danes and Tom Hiddleston, whose performances set the show apart. Beyond its recognizable leading duo, the series distinguishes itself with a slowly building tension as the story unfolds and shifts, making it impossible to stop watching. Consisting of just six episodes, The Essex Serpent is an easy binge for genre fans that will not disappoint.

The Essex Serpent is a Victorian-era gothic romance centered around Cora Seaborne (Danes), a newly widowed woman and a paleontology enthusiast. Rather than mourning the death of her abusive husband, Cora uses her newfound freedom to indulge her scientific interests by investigating reports of the Essex Serpent, a mythical creature said to be preying on the village of Aldwinter. Packing up her son, Frankie (Michael Jibson), and her companion, Martha (Hayley Squires), Cora travels to Aldwinter to see what she can uncover.

There can never be too many period dramas.

Once in Aldwinter, Cora learns that the residents fully believe the creature is responsible for local tragedies — except for the village's skeptical vicar, Will (Hiddleston), who forges an intellectual bond with Cora. The possibility of anything more is risky, considering Will is already married to Stella (Clémence Poésy), yet the love triangle that forms between them holds its own surprises. As locals disappear and disaster mounts, the villagers grow suspicious of Cora despite her attempts to help. Rather than the creature story it initially seems to be, The Essex Serpent focuses on deeper themes to create a character-driven narrative with slowly building tension.

The Essex Serpent's main narrative obstacle isn't the potential existence of the titular creature itself, but the dangerous hysteria in Aldwinter as well as the complicated romance between Cora and Will, both of which take time to build. Ultimately, the characters are the story's greatest strength, with the series relying on their complex emotional journeys to carry the show. Thanks to the talented cast, especially standouts Danes and Hiddleston, the miniseries explores layered people that make The Essex Serpent a rewarding watch.

Four love stories, four completely different ideas of what it means to be somebody's person. This is the one that matches how you actually fall, and what it would ask of you.

You do not fall for strangers. You fall for the person you have already been talking to for years, which is the slowest and by some distance the most durable way anybody has found to do this.

You want a partner, not an audience. Somebody who wants their own life as badly as you want yours, and who would never ask you to be smaller to make the relationship easier.

The intensity was never the problem. The secrecy is. You are drawn to a love that lives entirely in private, and to the particular ache of wanting somebody you are not supposed to want.

You do not do this cautiously. When it happens for you it is immediate and total and it rearranges everything, and you would not accept a smaller version of it.

While there's plenty to set The Essex Serpent apart, Danes' character is a highlight. Subverting the expectations of a traditional Victorian heroine, Cora's deep belief in science and natural skepticism make her compelling to watch. Additionally, the story makes no secret of her abusive marriage, but allows her to escape following her husband's death and pursue her own intellectual interests. The Essex Serpent may have been overlooked, but it isn't lacking in surprising twists and turns, and its short format rewards any period drama fan looking for a thrilling weekend binge.

Netflix’s Franchise-Ending Thriller Officially Becomes a Global Streaming Hit

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Horror and Netflix go hand in hand, especially during spooky season. This is best exemplified by the streamer's acquisition of the biggest horror movie of the year, scheduled to be added to the catalog on November 17. As an Oscars campaign for Best Actress begins to take shape for Inde Navarrette, even more eyes than before will get to witness the sheer breakout genius of director Curry Barker's Obsession. The movie earned widespread praise from critics, becoming one of the highest-rated horror films of the year and an outside favorite for awards season.

But Obsession's biggest success came at the box office, where it surprised the world by turning a sub-$1 million budget into more than half a billion, breaking numerous records in the process. Obsession became the highest-grossing non-animated original film of the past decade, the highest-grossing original horror movie of this century, the fifth highest-grossing horror film of all time, and much more during its memorable theatrical run. Very soon, it will become the horror to watch on Netflix, but what is holding the fort in the meantime?

At the time of writing, the controversial Halloween Ends is one of the ten most-watched movies on Netflix in the U.S. The conclusion to David Gordon Green's modern sequel trilogy to John Carpenter’s slasher classic, Halloween Ends marked Jamie Lee Curtis' final appearance as Laurie Strode, but sadly didn't see the actress go out in glory. "Halloween Ends seems to be an attempt to give Halloween fans something new, something unique, and something a little different from what they had seen before, and unfortunately, it doesn't work," wrote Alyse Wax in Collider's review at the time.

It might've earned some pretty harsh reviews, but Halloween Ends ​​​​​​at least proved popular at the box office. Against a reported budget of $20 million, the film scored a global haul of $105 million, split between $64 million in domestic revenue and a further $41 million from overseas markets. The film opened at the top spot in the domestic ranks, outperforming horror rival Smile during Halloween season. Alas, poor word of mouth ended in an 80% drop in ticket sales by weekend #2, and a drop out of the top ten within five weeks.

The 10 Best Survival Movies of All Time, Ranked

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Technically, a whole bunch of movies are about survival, but a line was drawn here to look at the ones really focused on surviving. Basically, no horror or action movies where people are obviously fighting to stay alive the whole time, because if you included anything with life-or-death stakes, then you'd probably find yourself with more “survival movies” on your hands than non-survival movies.

The ones below are mostly adventure/drama films, with some creeping into other territories, but as long as characters are isolated and battling the elements (more so than specific disasters) in one way or another (and maybe some other threats, too, at least on the side), then they're worth considering as survival movies. And they're high-quality, too, and definitely worthy of being considered among the best survival movies ever made.

Rescue Dawn takes place during the Vietnam War, so maybe it’s more of a war movie early on, but the focus is definitely on survival and less about the usual stuff you'd find in more straightforward war movies. It’s based on the real-life experiences of a pilot named Dieter Dengler, and what happened when his plane got shot down over Laos, with him having to battle the elements and coming into contact with forces who make him a prisoner of war, too.

There’s time spent on surviving time in a POW camp on top of having to survive (and trek through) jungle terrain, so Rescue Dawn is certainly grueling, but by design. It’s one of the better non-documentaries Werner Herzog has directed, though he did also cover the same story in a documentary, and that film’s pretty great as well (it was made about a decade earlier and was called Little Dieter Needs to Fly).

This is one you sort of have to mention, because it’s probably one of the first things most people think about when they hear the term “survival movie,” but yes, it’s Cast Away. There’s a desert island, there’s just the one guy stranded on it, and he has to stay alive on that island for an extended period of time. It’s all very stripped back in terms of the premise, but there are things beyond it being just literally a guy and an island for nearly 2.5 hours.

Still, the bulk of the movie is that. And a little under 2.5 hours is long, but Cast Away stays interesting for most of that runtime. It’s one of the good Robert Zemeckis films, which is always nice to see (when he’s on, he’s on, but he definitely isn't always on, y’know?), and it’s also got one of many strong Tom Hanks performances at its center, so that dynamic director/actor duo ensures the movie’s pretty good for the most part.

Cast Away but it’s Mars instead of an island: that’s The Martian. Kind of. Someone is alone for a good chunk of the movie, and they have to figure out ways to survive their predicament while also needing to think about how they might actually get out of the situation they're in, with help from anyone else being limited, at least at first.

The Martian works well as a crowd-pleaser, not exactly doing a ton to rewrite the survival sub-genre, but hitting all the beats in a more-than-solid manner.

It’s also one of a fair few movies that features a character played by Matt Damon trying to get home in one way or another, so that’s possibly worthy of note. And The Martian works well as a crowd-pleaser, not exactly doing a ton to rewrite the survival sub-genre, but hitting all the beats in a more-than-solid manner and, overall, ending up being a pretty difficult-to-dislike sort of film.

If you're picking up a theme here, with stories about one person battling the elements or otherwise being in a stressful situation while alone, then congrats. But also not really congrats, because it’s obvious. That’s a lot of survival movies. And that is also All Is Lost, though maybe this one goes a step further because the only cast member listed is Robert Redford.

The closest thing to a second character here is the boat he’s on throughout the film, and even then, he’s not a particularly well-developed character, not even having a confirmed name or anything. Nothing else is really relevant, though. There’s been an accident, the boat is sinking, and he doesn’t want to drown. That’s enough for All Is Lost, because it’s genuinely quite gripping throughout, and it’s one of the best of the late-era Robert Redford movies, too (he does a lot considering he was nearing 80 at the time of this film’s release, though there is one other survival movie he starred in decades earlier that’s also worth mentioning… more on that one in a bit).

What do you know: Buried is about a guy who gets buried. No clickbait with this title, that’s for sure. Ryan Reynolds is that guy, and he gives a surprisingly good performance here in a movie pretty much devoid of anything funny or otherwise light-hearted, since he’s stuck in a coffin the whole time, well and truly, you know, buried, and he has a limited amount of time to try and contact someone who can then work out where he is.

He’s really just equipped with a phone and a lighter, so he’s calling people, stressing out, and exploring any option he can for the 90 minutes of oxygen he has (approximately), and that is also the length of the movie. So, Buried plays out basically in real-time, and it’s remarkably intense for all of those minutes, successfully stretching out that very memorable coffin scene from Kill Bill: Vol. 2 into a feature-length film.

This is a tricky one to talk about, because Deliverance is a deeply unpleasant movie, but it takes a little while to get to that territory, and then also, discussing that stuff feels like it could be a bit much on top of it possibly involving saying too much, in terms of the narrative and all that. But then again, it’s more than half a century old at this point, so you probably know at least a little about this movie and why it’s notorious, even if you’ve not actually seen it.

If you don’t mind the idea of having a bad time with a good movie, it’s likely worth watching. Deliverance does make you feel the desperation of its main characters uncomfortably well, and there’s undeniable skill involved here with it all building the way it does. And a movie being this old and still feeling shocking in many ways is quite impressive when you think about it, too.

Just over 40 years before All Is Lost, Robert Redford also starred in Jeremiah Johnson, which is a different sort of adventure/survival movie, sure, but both are great, in any event. Jeremiah Johnson definitely isn't as minimalist, since there are a few other characters here, and the main character isn't as restricted, not being stuck out at sea and instead having to survive in and around the Rocky Mountains.

It’s also set back in the mid-1800s, very much being a Western, but one focused on survival, and then there is some additional conflict in the movie involving some other people in the area the titular character lives. Still, not a lot of story. It’s not minimalist compared to All Is Lost, but placed against most other more “conventional” movies, it’s very light on story, but not in a bad way. It’s interesting to watch something that’s kind of a hang-out movie, but lots of it is just the one guy, rather than a film where you get to see, like, a group of characters hang out and everything.

Take the setting and the chilly Western feel of Jeremiah Johnson and add in the grit/violence of Deliverance, and you'd find yourself with something that might be honestly kind of close to The Revenant. Okay, not perfectly, but those itches scratched by those films get scratched here, and also, both those movies from the early 1970s have revenge play some kind of role in their respective stories.

The Revenant is as much a revenge movie as it is one about survival, with one man seemingly staying alive against all odds so he can brutally murder the man who wronged him. That’s a simple way to break down such a long film, yet that is pretty much what The Revenant boils down to. It tells that straightforward story well, though, all the while doing some interesting things thematically and feeling hard to fault in terms of its technical aspects and also regarding many of the performances it features.

Of all the notably uplifting movies out there, 127 Hours could well be the most disturbing, but it has to be disturbing and grisly before it can get uplifting. It feels right, in other words, and while “that” scene that everyone knows might be nauseating to watch without context, if you see it in the movie, at the point at which it happens… well, sure, it’s still nauseating, but it’s also undeniably triumphant.

This one came out the same year as Buried, and it’s similarly claustrophobic, all the while also being much more visually unpleasant and then, somehow not paradoxically, also a whole lot less depressing. 127 Hours is about as good as the more mundane survival movies out there get, because it’s easy to imagine yourself in this situation, get some kind of understanding about the pain involved, yet also then find some inspiration in how far a real-life person was willing to go to survive.

Gravity is not based on a real-life story, but it certainly feels real while you're watching it, and it’s also grounded in enough ways to make it a bit difficult to classify as a science fiction movie. It’s about survival in space, but the disaster, in effect, isn't too outlandish, and then it also doesn’t happen very far into space, by any means, with Earth always very much in view, but still undeniably difficult to get back to in one piece.

Also, Gravity is very heavy on spectacle for many of its scenes, though, things are also personal and character-focused, to some extent, with enough time to spend on the characters even with a short runtime because there are so few characters. Some people find parts of Gravity silly and maybe a little overly sentimental, too, but it is so spectacular and laser-focused on survival throughout that it feels worthy of being ranked as high as it is here. If you can get on board with what this movie’s doing both technically and emotionally, it really is one of the most thrilling and involving films out there.

New R-Rated DC Movie Officially Replaces Bruce Wayne As Batman Next Month

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

DC has a surprisingly rich history of replacing Bruce Wayne as Batman. Famously, Terry McGinnis took up the cowl in Batman Beyond, proving pretty resoundingly that Gotham’s dark future could thrive under the shadow of a new Dark Knight, and he’s far from the only one.

The main DC comic continuity also has seen plenty of familiar faces pull on Wayne’s cowl, too: Dick Grayson, Tim Drake, and Damian Wayne have all taken a turn wrestling with the weight of the bat symbol. Even Jim Gordon and Alfred have temporarily become Gotham’s masked protector, while Jace Fox followed Terry McGinnis as a legitimate heir before shifting his operation to New York City. In other words, the supposed sanctity of Bruce Wayne as Batman has been interrupted a number of times.

The most notorious example, though, came in the wake of Bruce Wayne’s most famous defeat at the muscular hands of Bane in DC’s mid-'90s Knightfall arc. DC has long discussed the impact of being Batman on Bruce, and how heavy the cape really is, but it comes into focus most prominently when he steps aside, and Knightfall showed that cost better than any story.

Following the brutal climax of Batman: Knightfall - Part 1, the upcoming release of Batman: Knightfall - Part 2: Knightquest will officially hand the cowl to Bruce Wayne’s most infamous replacement. And while passing the torch often feels like a triumphant rite of passage, Knightfall is actually a terrifying warning about what happens when the wrong man tries to play god.

The ending of Knightfall: Part 1 delivered one of the most brutal cliffhangers in DC history as Bane systematically tore Batman apart and then snapped his spine. Played with dark relish by Michael Mando, the hulking DC villain was the real deal, and that climax was the perfect way to shatter the illusion of Batman’s invincibility. He did it to prove his superiority, as well as finally overcoming his fear of bats.

That same moment has been shown on screen before, of course, as Christopher Nolan partially adapted Knightfall into The Dark Knight Rises to send Christian Bale’s Batman spiraling to his lowest point of the trilogy. Tom Hardy plays a very different Bane, of course, and the twist in The Dark Knight Rise’s ending changes him further, but the image of the giant brute snapping Batman over his knee was still iconic.

Interestingly, Nolan’s final Batman movie actually used Batman’s vulnerability as the way in to tell a very different story than Knightfall. For Nolan, Bruce passing the torch (to Joseph Gordon-Levitt’s Blake) was important to underline the idea that Batman as a symbol was more important than Bruce Wayne’s destructive commitment to his duties. Nolan chose to give his Batman a happy ending by freeing him after Bane’s victory over him, but in Knightfall, the terrifying inverse of Nolan's philosophy is true.

The original comic storyline - and 2026’s excellent and faithful R-rated animated adaptation - confirms instead that Batman is not an off-the-shelf symbol that anyone can simply step into. The cowl requires restraint, empathy, and psychological discipline, and Bruce Wayne is very much presented as the only viable option to keep Gotham safe. Including from Batman.

When Bruce is paralyzed and forced off the streets, you might naturally expect that Dick Grayson would step up as his natural heir. But Bruce deliberately overlooked him, partly because DC wanted to protect the successful separation of Nightwing, but also for narrative reasons. They were somewhat estranged for a start, but Bruce also saw an opportunity to further redeem Jean-Paul Valley, the former assassin, by giving him the Batman mantle.

Big mistake. Batman’s broken legacy being added to the volatile background of Azrael was easily one of Bruce’s most idiotic moves. He was a brainwashed child killer, conditioned to kill by the System, and Batman’s strict moral code and no-kill rule simply doesn’t fit with how he’s built. He modifies the Batsuit into one of the coolest versions ever, thanks to DC’s desire to parody dark 1990s extreme antihero tropes, and he effectively becomes a monstrous, heavily weaponized mirror of Batman, instead of Valley changing himself.

As Knightfall Part 2 will show, Valley’s extreme measures and aggressive style did deliver results, and he’s able to stand up to Bane in his new suit, after initially suffering a defeat that drives him to make the improvements. But it all comes at a cost, and in contrast to Nolan’s message in The Dark Knight Rises, it becomes clear that Bruce Wayne brings something to being Batman that stops the hero becoming Gotham’s nightmare.

Batman: Knightfall Part 2: Knightquest will release on December 8.

Godzilla Minus Zero Opening Weekend Box Office Breaks A Foreign-Language Film Records

Godzilla Minus Zero had a great first weekend in theaters

Godzilla Minus Zero had a strong opening weekend, grossing more at the domestic box office than any live action foreign-language film before it. On top of that, a higher percentage of people sought out premium screens, like IMAX, to watch it even more than they did for The Odyssey earlier this year.

Deadline's reporting highlights that Godzilla Minus Zero is estimated to have grossed $32 million. Although American-made Godzilla movies have made more during their first weekend in theaters, this is a very impressive feat for a foreign-language film, as they rarely do this well at the domestic box office unless they're connected to an anime. As mentioned, this opening is record-breaking for a live-action one.

Godzilla Minus Zero's performance also shows the value of making movies that people want to see on the biggest screen possible, as around 74% of its gross came from IMAX or other premium large format screens. In comparison, The Odyssey got a lot of attention because around 54% of its opening weekend gross came from PLF screens. 2026 has certainly shown that there's demand for blockbusters that embrace that opulent style of filmmaking beyond a typical superhero movie.

Outside of Godzilla Minus Zero, the animated Cat in the Hat movie had a fairly underwhelming $20 million opening weekend, while You Can See Everything had an impressive hold week-over-week that makes a $100 million domestic gross more possible. That said, we do still seem to be in the quiet before the storm as new blockbuster Hunger Games, Avengers, and Dune movies hit theaters before the end of the year
